the current interpretation remains the authoritative reference interpretation until explicitly revised;
downstream research may question, test, contradict, or explicitly depart from it;
silent substitution of another position remains prohibited.
Preserve D1:

the researcher’s interpretation may evolve;
prior interpretations are preserved as historical provenance;
at any given time, one interpretation constitutes the current authoritative understanding of a concept.
Do not assume every new input automatically requires interpretation revision.
Distinguish:

reconsideration;
actual interpretation change.
Do not invent a completeness requirement over unknown or undiscovered material.
Use the bounded available research state established by A6.

Required adversarial tests must include:

A new materially relevant claim that directly conflicts with the current interpretation.
A new claim that is relevant but merely duplicates existing support.
Newly discovered context that changes the meaning or applicability of a foundational claim.
A5 reclassifies a prior tension as a genuine contradiction.
New empirical evidence contradicts the current interpretation.
Weak or inconclusive empirical results appear.
The researcher changes their mind after re-reading the exact same unchanged claim landscape.
The researcher changes preference with no new reason or substantive re-evaluation.
Downstream research explicitly departs from the current interpretation without changing it.
New material affects a peripheral aspect of the concept but not the interpretation foundation.
Required output structure:

Exact Semantic Distinctions
Minimum Options
Trigger Determination Framework
Binding Methodology Rules
Adversarial Tests
Recommended Decision
What A7 Unlocks
Remaining Questions — Classified Only
Final Verdict
Stop Statement
Status must be:

PROPOSED — awaiting semantic consistency audit

Introduce no new entities, tables, workflows, architecture, schema, cardinalities, or implementation mechanisms.

# DECISION A6 — RESEARCHER INTERPRETATION CONSTRUCTION

**Status:** RATIFIED
**Upstream Authority:**
1. PROJECT MINORE — FOUNDING DEFINITION v0.1
2. DECISION D1 — CONCEPT UNDERSTANDING: RESEARCHER INTERPRETATION LIFECYCLE
3. DECISION A1 — RESEARCH INPUT ADMISSIBILITY AND PROVENANCE
4. DECISION A2 — CLAIM EXTRACTION PROCEDURE
5. A2-C1 and A2-C2 (BINDING)
6. DECISION A3 — CLAIM CLASSIFICATION
7. A3-C1 (BINDING)
8. DECISION A4 — CONCEPT ASSOCIATION
9. DECISION A5 — CONTRADICTION HANDLING PROCEDURE

---

## 1. Exact Semantic Distinctions

| Term | Meaning |
|------|---------|
| **Source claim** | An extracted, classified, and concept-associated claim attributed to a research input source (A1–A4). Immutable and attributable per D1. |
| **Available claim landscape** | The set of source claims currently associated with a concept within the admitted and processed research state, including all identified differences, variations, tensions, and genuine contradictions per A5. |
| **Researcher interpretation** | The researcher's current authoritative understanding of a concept, constructed from the available claim landscape. It represents what the researcher currently believes about the concept. |
| **Interpretation foundation** | The subset of source claims and reasoning from the available claim landscape that directly inform the researcher's interpretation. |
| **Interpretation reasoning** | The explicit logical or evidential reasoning by which the researcher arrived at their interpretation from the available claim landscape. |
| **Source-derived interpretation** | An interpretation that directly reflects or synthesizes what sources explicitly stated. |
| **Researcher synthesis** | An interpretation that synthesizes, integrates, or generalizes from multiple source claims in ways that go beyond any single source's explicit assertion. |
| **Provisional interpretation** | An interpretation that explicitly acknowledges uncertainty, unresolved contradiction, or pending empirical evidence. |
| **Authoritative interpretation** | The researcher's current, formally adopted position on what a concept means or encompasses. It is the reference interpretation for downstream research work until the researcher explicitly revises it. |

---

## 2. Minimum Options

### Option A — Source Consensus Required
The researcher may construct an interpretation only when all associated source claims agree or can be fully reconciled.

**Problem:** Directly contradicts D1 Rule 5 and A5 (which explicitly permit unresolved contradictions). Blocks interpretation construction whenever disagreement exists. Fails to answer: how does the researcher proceed with concept work when sources conflict?

### Option B — Researcher Selection without Constraint
The researcher may construct any interpretation without requirement to account for or acknowledge the available claim landscape.

**Problem:** Violates D1's requirement to preserve reasoning chain (D1 Capability 13). Interpretation becomes arbitrary; provenance is lost. Researcher interpretation would be indistinguishable from source claims.

### Option C — Constrained Researcher Authority with Reasoning Requirement
The researcher constructs an interpretation from the available claim landscape while required to:
- Account for all materially relevant claims in the available landscape (avoid silent dismissal of evidence)
- Explicitly reason from the claim landscape
- Preserve the full reasoning chain
- Acknowledge unresolved contradictions or tensions when present
- Permit provisional interpretations that acknowledge uncertainty

**Test against D1:** Preserves researcher authority (D1 Rule 2), permits evolution (D1 Rule 3), requires provenance chain (D1 Capability 13), permits unresolved contradictions (D1 Rule 5).

**Test against A5:** Respects contradiction determination; does not force false reconciliation.

**Verdict:** Option C is the minimum viable and founding-consistent standard.

---

## 3. Interpretation Construction Procedure

The following procedure governs how the researcher constructs an authoritative interpretation of a concept from the available claim landscape.

**Step 1 — Gather the available claim landscape**
Identify all extracted source claims currently associated with the concept per A4. Include all identified differences, variations, tensions, and genuine contradictions per A5.

**Step 2 — Assess the claim landscape**
Determine:
- Are all claims in broad agreement? (High consensus case)
- Are there compatible variations (different scope, context, emphasis)?
- Are there unresolved tensions (difficulty of reconciliation without full resolution)?
- Are there genuine unresolved contradictions?
- Are some claims more relevant to core concept definition than others?

**Step 3 — Determine the interpretation foundation**
Select which claims will directly inform the interpretation. This selection must be explicit and reasoned.

Selection may exclude claims based on:
- Relevance to core concept vs. peripheral variations
- Scope or applicability conditions
- Competing claims where the researcher judges one to be more foundational to the concept (with explicit reasoning)

Selection may **not** exclude claims based on:
- Silent dismissal without acknowledgment
- Mere disagreement with researcher preference without substantive reasoning
- Source authority alone (per A5 Rule 8)

**Step 4 — Construct the interpretation**
Formulate the researcher's authoritative understanding of the concept. The interpretation may:

a) **Reflect source consensus** — if all material claims agree, state the interpretation as consensus-derived

b) **Synthesize compatible variations** — if claims are variations on a compatible theme, synthesize into a unified understanding that encompasses the variations

c) **Acknowledge unresolved tension** — if claims create tension without strict incompatibility, construct an interpretation that acknowledges the tension explicitly

d) **Bracket genuine contradiction** — if genuine unresolved contradictions exist, construct an interpretation that provisionally adopts a position while explicitly preserving the contradiction

e) **Remain provisional** — acknowledge that the interpretation is provisional pending resolution of contradiction, additional source material, or empirical evidence

The interpretation may take the following forms:
- Consensus: "All sources agree that [concept] means [X]"
- Synthesized variation: "Sources describe [concept] with different emphases on [variations]. The interpretation integrates these as [synthesis]"
- Acknowledged tension: "Sources present [concept] with some interpretive difficulty between [positions]. The interpretation provisionally adopts [position] while noting the tension"
- Bracketed contradiction: "Sources conflict on whether [concept] requires [property]. The interpretation provisionally adopts [position] and preserves the unresolved contradiction"
- Provisional: "The interpretation of [concept] is [X], provisionally pending [specific uncertainty/evidence]"

**Step 5 — Document the reasoning chain**
Preserve explicit connection between:
- The interpretation adopted
- The available claim landscape considered
- Materiality judgments about which claims were foundational
- Treatment of contradictions and tensions
- Any explicit acknowledgment of uncertainty or provisionality

**Step 6 — Establish interpretation as authoritative**
The researcher formally adopts this interpretation as their current understanding of the concept. This interpretation is the authoritative reference interpretation for downstream research work unless explicitly revised.

---

## 4. Binding Methodology Rules

1. **Researcher interpretation is the researcher's authoritative current position on what a concept means or encompasses.** It is distinct from source claims (immutable, attributable) and from empirical validation (tested later). It represents the researcher's reasoned judgment.

2. **Interpretation must account for the available claim landscape.** The researcher must consider all materially relevant source claims in the available claim landscape. Silent dismissal of available materially relevant claims is prohibited.

3. **Interpretation foundation must be explicit.** The researcher must state which claims form the foundation of the interpretation and why. If claims are excluded from the foundation, the exclusion must be reasoned.

4. **Researcher authority permits interpretation beyond source consensus.** The researcher may construct interpretations that:
   - Synthesize multiple source claims into unified understanding not explicitly stated by any single source
   - Adopt a position in response to tension or contradiction, making an authoritative choice
   - Acknowledge provisionality and unresolved uncertainty

5. **Unresolved contradictions must not be silently reconciled.** If A5 identifies a genuine unresolved contradiction, the interpretation may provisionally adopt a position, but the contradiction must be explicitly preserved in the interpretation reasoning chain.

6. **Interpretations may be provisional.** An interpretation may explicitly acknowledge:
   - Unresolved contradiction in the source landscape
   - Pending empirical validation
   - Uncertainty pending additional source material
   - Tension requiring future resolution

A provisional interpretation is still the authoritative reference interpretation for downstream work unless explicitly revised.

7. **Interpretation does not alter source claims.** Per D1, source claims remain immutable and attributable. Interpretation is constructed from claims, not by rewriting them.

8. **Interpretation reasoning must preserve the full chain per D1 Capability 13.** The reasoning connecting interpretation to the available claim landscape must be explicit and traceable.

9. **Interpretation is not empirical validation.** The researcher's interpretation of what a concept means is distinct from evidence that the concept is true or effective in markets. Later empirical testing (M2, M3) may support or contradict the interpretation without invalidating the interpretation itself.

10. **Source authority does not override reasoning.** Per A5 Rule 8, a claim from a highly reputable source does not automatically become the interpretation foundation. The researcher's reasoning must justify the choice.

11. **Interpretation synthesis must preserve the semantic roles of claims as classified per A3.** Mechanistic and Predictive claims may inform concept understanding without being transformed into Definitional claims. When synthesizing claims of different types, the interpretation must make clear which aspects are definitional, which are mechanistic, and which are predictive.

12. **The current interpretation is the authoritative reference interpretation for downstream research work.** Downstream research references this interpretation unless explicitly departing with documented reasoning. Downstream research may question, test, contradict, or depart from the current interpretation, but may not silently treat another position as authoritative.

---

## 5. Adversarial Tests

**Test 1 — Broad agreement across sources**

Concept: Fair Value Gap
Source Claims:
- Claim A (Source 1, Definitional): "A Fair Value Gap is a three-candle imbalance where candles 1 and 3 wicks do not overlap"
- Claim B (Source 2, Mechanistic): "Fair Value Gaps are price inefficiencies created when price moves without trading in a zone"
- Claim C (Source 3, Definitional): "FVGs form when price gaps over support/resistance levels without filling the gap"

Claim Landscape Assessment:
- Claim A: Structural definition (Definitional)
- Claim B: Mechanism description (Mechanistic)
- Claim C: Formation condition at support/resistance (Definitional)
- All three are compatible; they describe different aspects of the same phenomenon

Interpretation Construction:
- Foundation: All three claims inform the core understanding
- Synthesis: "A Fair Value Gap is defined structurally as a three-candle imbalance where candles 1 and 3 wicks do not overlap (Definitional, Claim A). Fair Value Gaps form when price gaps over support/resistance levels (Definitional, Claim C). This structural pattern represents a price inefficiency mechanism where price moves without trading in a zone (Mechanistic, Claim B). The interpretation integrates these three complementary aspects while preserving their distinct semantic roles."
- Provisional status: Not provisional; consensus across sources
- Framework handling: ✓ Synthesizes multiple compatible claims into unified interpretation while preserving A3 semantic roles

---

**Test 2 — Multiple compatible variations**

Concept: Order Block
Source Claims:
- Claim A (Source 1): "Order Blocks form from bearish consolidation before bullish impulses"
- Claim B (Source 2): "Order Blocks form from the last consolidation before any impulse, regardless of direction"
- Claim C (Source 3): "Order Blocks are consolidations that precede significant price movements; the specific impulse direction varies"

Claim Landscape Assessment:
- Claim A: Specific (bearish consolidation → bullish impulse)
- Claim B: General (consolidation → any impulse)
- Claim C: General with variations
- Tension: Claim A is more specific; Claims B and C are broader. Not contradictory, but varying scope

Interpretation Construction:
- Foundation: All three claims; Claim A is more specific, Claims B and C capture breadth
- Synthesis: "An Order Block is a consolidation that precedes an impulse. While impulses can be directional (Claim A's bearish-before-bullish pattern) or bidirectional (Claims B, C), the core definition is the consolidation-impulse relationship. Sources suggest the bearish-to-bullish pattern is common but not universal."
- Provisional status: Not provisional; the variation is resolvable as scope differences
- Framework handling: ✓ Interpretation encompasses variations by distinguishing core definition from scope variations

---

**Test 3 — Unresolved contradiction**

Concept: Optimal Trade Entry
Source Claims:
- Claim A (Source 1): "Optimal Trade Entry occurs at the 61.8% retracement of the impulse"
- Claim B (Source 2): "Optimal Trade Entry occurs at the 50% retracement"
- Claim C (Source 3): "Optimal Trade Entry varies based on impulse strength; typically 50–70% retracement"

Claim Landscape Assessment:
- Claim A and B: Conflicting specific numbers (61.8% vs. 50%)
- Claim C: Range acknowledgment
- Tension: Multiple sources assert different specific entry levels; Claim C acknowledges variation but does not fully resolve the conflict

Contradiction Analysis (A5): Is this a genuine contradiction?
- Claim A asserts 61.8% specifically
- Claim B asserts 50% specifically
- If applied to the same impulse under the same conditions, both cannot be optimal
- Genuine contradiction (unresolved per A5)

Interpretation Construction:
- Foundation: All three claims; acknowledge that sources conflict on the specific level
- Interpretation (provisional): "Optimal Trade Entry is provisionally interpreted as occurring in the 50–70% retracement range (Claim C), which encompasses but does not resolve the contradiction between 50% (Claim B) and 61.8% (Claim A). The interpretation adopts the broader range while explicitly preserving the unresolved contradiction about the specific optimal level. Further empirical testing or source clarification is required to resolve which specific level, if either, is optimal."
- Provisional status: Yes — explicitly acknowledges the unresolved contradiction
- Framework handling: ✓ Interpretation acknowledges unresolved contradiction without forcing false reconciliation; remains authoritative despite provisionality

---

**Test 4 — Genuine unresolved contradiction**

Concept: Market Maker Intent
Source Claims:
- Claim A (Source 1): "Market Makers actively hunt stop-losses to accumulate liquidity for their trades"
- Claim B (Source 2): "Market Makers' stop-loss hunting is a side effect of their natural order flow, not intentional"

Claim Landscape Assessment:
- Direct logical incompatibility: intentional vs. unintentional behavior
- Applied to the same market conditions and same Market Makers
- No context resolves this contradiction (A5 Step 2)
- Genuine unresolved contradiction per A5

Interpretation Construction:
- Foundation: Both claims present; researcher acknowledges the contradiction
- Interpretation (bracketed contradiction): "The interpretation of Market Maker Intent is that Market Makers' behavior creates stop-loss hunting effects (both sources agree on the behavior). However, sources disagree on whether this is intentional (Claim A) or a side effect of natural order flow (Claim B). The interpretation provisionally treats it as intentional behavior while explicitly preserving the contradiction about intent."
- Provisional status: Yes — explicitly brackets and preserves the unresolved contradiction
- Researcher choice: The researcher has selected intentionality as the provisional foundation, but justified the choice by acknowledging the competing claim
- Framework handling: ✓ Interpretation does not force reconciliation; does not silently dismiss Claim B; makes an authoritative choice while preserving the contradiction

---

**Test 5 — Researcher selecting one interpretation over competing claims**

Concept: Liquidity Void
Source Claims:
- Claim A (Source 1, highly reputable): "Liquidity Voids are zones without market participants; price will eventually fill them"
- Claim B (Source 2, lower provenance confidence): "Liquidity Voids are temporary phenomena that often close without price filling the zone"

Claim Landscape Assessment:
- Competing claims about the same phenomenon
- A5 status: For the purpose of this test, assume A5 classified the claims as Tension rather than Genuine Contradiction.
- Tension: Source A asserts voids will fill; Source B asserts they often close without filling
- Source A has higher authority/reputation

Interpretation Construction (NOT RECOMMENDED):
- If reasoning is: "Source A is more reputable, therefore adopt Claim A as interpretation"
- Framework handling: ✗ Violates Rule 10 (source authority does not override reasoning)

Interpretation Construction (CORRECT):
- Foundation: Acknowledge both claims; reason from available landscape
- Interpretation: "Liquidity Voids are zones without market participants. The interpretation acknowledges unresolved tension in source material: Source A asserts they eventually fill; Source B asserts they often close without filling. The interpretation provisionally adopts the position that Liquidity Voids will eventually fill (Claim A), while explicitly preserving the tension with Claim B's assertion that they often close without filling. This provisional interpretation awaits empirical testing to determine under what conditions voids fill versus close without filling."
- Provisional status: Yes — explicitly preserves the tension
- Framework handling: ✓ Researcher makes a choice with explicit reasoning; preserves competing claim; does not manufacture contextual qualifiers absent from available claims

---

**Test 6 — Researcher constructing interpretation beyond what any single source states**

Concept: Institutional Trading Pattern Recognition
Source Claims:
- Claim A (Source 1): "Institutions create Order Blocks through consolidation"
- Claim B (Source 2): "Institutions create Fair Value Gaps through rapid price movement"
- Claim C (Source 3): "Institutions use both consolidation and rapid movement to manage liquidity"

Claim Landscape Assessment:
- All three claims are compatible; each describes part of institutional behavior
- No single source states a complete integrated framework
- Researcher synthesis is possible and appropriate

Interpretation Construction:
- Foundation: All three claims; researcher integrates them
- Synthesis (researcher-derived): "Institutional Trading Pattern Recognition in this framework encompasses two primary mechanisms: Order Blocks (consolidation-based liquidity management, Claim A) and Fair Value Gaps (movement-based liquidity management, Claim B). These represent complementary tactics within an integrated liquidity-management strategy (Claim C). The interpretation synthesizes these into a two-mechanism framework."
- Provisional status: Not provisional; synthesis is well-supported by the available claim landscape
- Researcher contribution: Integration of three distinct claims into a unified framework
- Framework handling: ✓ Researcher goes beyond single-source assertion but remains grounded in available claim landscape; reasoning is traceable and authoritative

---

**Test 7 — New relevant source material appearing after interpretation**

Researcher has established interpretation (from Test 6):
"Institutional Trading Pattern Recognition encompasses Order Blocks and Fair Value Gaps as complementary liquidity-management mechanisms."

New Source Material:
- Claim D (Source 4, discovered after interpretation): "Institutions also use psychological price levels (round numbers) as a third liquidity-management mechanism, distinct from Order Blocks and FVGs"

Interpretation Evolution Trigger:
- New material is materially relevant (addresses institutional patterns)
- New material is domain-relevant per A2-C1
- New material cannot be ignored without violating Rule 2 (account for available claim landscape)
- This situation triggers a need for interpretation evolution

Framework handling: ✓ A6 acknowledges that new materially relevant source material creates a condition where interpretation evolution becomes appropriate. The procedure and triggers for such evolution are A7 territory.

---

**Test 8 — Researcher changing interpretation without new source material**

Researcher's Prior Interpretation:
"Order Blocks require bearish consolidation before bullish impulses"

Researcher's Revised Interpretation (same sources):
"Order Blocks form from any consolidation before any impulse direction; the bearish-before-bullish pattern is frequent but not universal"

Change Basis:
- Not new source material
- Re-examination of existing available claim landscape
- Researcher has shifted synthesis of existing claims (Test 2 situation)
- No new evidence, no new sources

Interpretation Evolution Trigger:
- This is a researcher judgment shift, not forced by new material
- Permitted per D1 Rule 3 (interpretation may evolve)
- The conditions and procedure for such evolution are A7 territory

Framework handling: ✓ A6 acknowledges that researcher re-examination of the available claim landscape may lead to judgment evolution. The triggers and procedure for formal revision are A7 territory.

---

## 6. Recommended Decision

**Option C — Constrained Researcher Authority with Reasoning Requirement**

The researcher constructs an authoritative interpretation from the available claim landscape with requirements to:
- Account for all materially relevant claims
- Explicitly reason from the claim landscape
- Preserve the full reasoning chain
- Acknowledge unresolved contradictions or tensions when present
- Permit provisional interpretations that acknowledge uncertainty

This model preserves researcher authority (D1 Rule 2), permits evolution (D1 Rule 3), requires provenance chain (D1 Capability 13), and permits unresolved contradictions (D1 Rule 5).

---

## 7. What A6 Unlocks

- **A7 — Interpretation Evolution Triggers** may now proceed, because the methodology establishes how interpretations are constructed and acknowledges that they may evolve; A7 will determine the conditions and procedures for formal interpretation revision.
- **B1 — Research-Question Identification** may now proceed, because the methodology can identify unanswered research questions within the established interpretation landscape.
- **B2 (testability criteria), M2 (hypothesis formation), and downstream research work** can now reference the researcher's authoritative reference interpretation of each concept as a foundation.

---

## 8. Remaining Questions — Classified Only

| Question | Classification |
|----------|---------------|
| Procedure for formally revising an interpretation | A7 territory — not A6 |
| Conditions triggering interpretation revision | A7 territory — not A6 |
| How interpretations are stored or versioned | DEFER TO ARCHITECTURE |
| Whether AI assists in interpretation construction | DEFER TO REQUIREMENTS |
| Weighting or confidence metrics for interpretations | DEFER TO ARCHITECTURE/REQUIREMENTS |
| Mechanism for formally adopting an interpretation as authoritative | Methodology content — not blocking A7 |
| Treatment of interpretations for association-pending claims (no established concept) | Methodology content — not blocking A7 |

## 9. Final Verdict

**DECISION A6 RATIFIED.**

Interpretation construction: Constrained researcher authority model with explicit reasoning requirement and available claim-landscape accountability.

Interpretation foundation: Selected from available claim landscape per explicit reasoning; unresolved contradictions and tensions preserved; provisional interpretations permitted.

Researcher authority: Permits synthesis beyond single-source claims and provisional positioning on contradictions; does not permit silent dismissal of materially relevant available claims or authority-based override of reasoning.

Relationship to ratified upstream: Consistent with D1 (researcher authority, interpretation evolution, reasoning chain), A1 (claim admissibility), A2 (claim extraction fidelity), A3 (classification semantic roles), A4 (concept association), A5 (contradiction preservation).

**Ratification basis:** Semantic Consistency Audit — eight corrections applied including: bounded available claim landscape; removed reliability weighting; replaced binding constraint with authoritative reference; preserved A3 semantic roles during synthesis; strengthened contradiction preservation in Test 3; inherited A5 status in Test 5; removed A7 scope overreach; removed question already resolved by D1.

**A7 — Interpretation Evolution Triggers is now unblocked but has not begun.**

## 3. Interpretation Construction Procedure

The following procedure governs how the researcher constructs an authoritative interpretation of a concept from the available claim landscape.

**Step 1 — Gather the available claim landscape**
Identify all extracted source claims currently associated with the concept per A4. Include all identified differences, variations, tensions, and genuine contradictions per A5.

**Step 2 — Assess the claim landscape**
Determine:
- Are all claims in broad agreement? (High consensus case)
- Are there compatible variations (different scope, context, emphasis)?
- Are there unresolved tensions (difficulty of reconciliation without full resolution)?
- Are there genuine unresolved contradictions?
- Are some claims more relevant to core concept definition than others?

**Step 3 — Determine the interpretation foundation**
Select which claims will directly inform the interpretation. This selection must be explicit and reasoned.

Selection may exclude claims based on:
- Relevance to core concept vs. peripheral variations
- Scope or applicability conditions
- Competing claims where the researcher judges one to be more foundational to the concept (with explicit reasoning)

Selection may **not** exclude claims based on:
- Silent dismissal without acknowledgment
- Mere disagreement with researcher preference without substantive reasoning
- Source authority alone (per A5 Rule 8)

**Step 4 — Construct the interpretation**
Formulate the researcher's authoritative understanding of the concept. The interpretation may:

a) **Reflect source consensus** — if all material claims agree, state the interpretation as consensus-derived

b) **Synthesize compatible variations** — if claims are variations on a compatible theme, synthesize into a unified understanding that encompasses the variations

c) **Acknowledge unresolved tension** — if claims create tension without strict incompatibility, construct an interpretation that acknowledges the tension explicitly

d) **Bracket genuine contradiction** — if genuine unresolved contradictions exist, construct an interpretation that provisionally adopts a position while explicitly preserving the contradiction

e) **Remain provisional** — acknowledge that the interpretation is provisional pending resolution of contradiction, additional source material, or empirical evidence

**Step 5 — Document the reasoning chain**
Preserve explicit connection between:
- The interpretation adopted
- The available claim landscape considered
- Materiality judgments about which claims were foundational
- Treatment of contradictions and tensions
- Any explicit acknowledgment of uncertainty or provisionality

**Step 6 — Establish interpretation as authoritative**
The researcher formally adopts this interpretation as their current understanding of the concept. This interpretation is the authoritative reference interpretation for downstream research work unless explicitly revised.

---

## 4. Binding Methodology Rules

1. **Researcher interpretation is the researcher's authoritative current position on what a concept means or encompasses.** It is distinct from source claims (immutable, attributable) and from empirical validation (tested later). It represents the researcher's reasoned judgment.

2. **Interpretation must account for the available claim landscape.** The researcher must consider all materially relevant source claims in the available claim landscape. Silent dismissal of available materially relevant claims is prohibited.

3. **Interpretation foundation must be explicit.** The researcher must state which claims form the foundation of the interpretation and why. If claims are excluded from the foundation, the exclusion must be reasoned.

4. **Researcher authority permits interpretation beyond source consensus.** The researcher may construct interpretations that:
   - Synthesize multiple source claims into unified understanding not explicitly stated by any single source
   - Adopt a position in response to tension or contradiction, making an authoritative choice
   - Acknowledge provisionality and unresolved uncertainty

5. **Unresolved contradictions must not be silently reconciled.** If A5 identifies a genuine unresolved contradiction, the interpretation may provisionally adopt a position, but the contradiction must be explicitly preserved in the interpretation reasoning chain.

6. **Interpretations may be provisional.** An interpretation may explicitly acknowledge:
   - Unresolved contradiction in the source landscape
   - Pending empirical validation
   - Uncertainty pending additional source material
   - Tension requiring future resolution

A provisional interpretation is still the authoritative reference interpretation for downstream work unless explicitly revised.

7. **Interpretation does not alter source claims.** Per D1, source claims remain immutable and attributable. Interpretation is constructed from claims, not by rewriting them.

8. **Interpretation reasoning must preserve the full chain per D1 Capability 13.** The reasoning connecting interpretation to the available claim landscape must be explicit and traceable.

9. **Interpretation is not empirical validation.** The researcher's interpretation of what a concept means is distinct from evidence that the concept is true or effective in markets. Later empirical testing (M2, M3) may support or contradict the interpretation without invalidating the interpretation itself.

10. **Source authority does not override reasoning.** Per A5 Rule 8, a claim from a highly reputable source does not automatically become the interpretation foundation. The researcher's reasoning must justify the choice.

11. **Interpretation synthesis must preserve the semantic roles of claims as classified per A3.** Mechanistic and Predictive claims may inform concept understanding without being transformed into Definitional claims. When synthesizing claims of different types, the interpretation must make clear which aspects are definitional, which are mechanistic, and which are predictive.

12. **The current interpretation is the authoritative reference interpretation for downstream research work.** Downstream research references this interpretation unless explicitly departing with documented reasoning. Downstream research may question, test, contradict, or depart from the current interpretation, but may not silently treat another position as authoritative.

---

## 5. Interpretation Construction Procedure — Detailed Application

**Step 1 input:** The available claim landscape for the concept

**Step 2 assessment:** Classify the landscape into categories (consensus, variations, tensions, contradictions)

**Step 3 foundation selection:**
- Identify core defining claims
- Identify peripheral or conditional claims
- Mark claims excluded from foundation (with explicit reasoning)
- Mark tensions and contradictions that will be addressed in Step 4

**Step 4 construction:** Formulate interpretation using one or more patterns:
- Consensus: "All sources agree that [concept] means [X]"
- Synthesized variation: "Sources describe [concept] with different emphases on [variations]. The interpretation integrates these as [synthesis]"
- Acknowledged tension: "Sources present [concept] with some interpretive difficulty between [positions]. The interpretation provisionally adopts [position] while noting the tension"
- Bracketed contradiction: "Sources conflict on whether [concept] requires [property]. The interpretation provisionally adopts [position] and preserves the unresolved contradiction"
- Provisional: "The interpretation of [concept] is [X], provisionally pending [specific uncertainty/evidence]"

**Step 5 output:** Documented reasoning chain connecting interpretation to available landscape

**Step 6 authorization:** Formal adoption as current authoritative reference interpretation

---

## 6. Adversarial Tests

**Test 1 — Broad agreement across sources**

Concept: Fair Value Gap
Source Claims:
- Claim A (Source 1, Definitional): "A Fair Value Gap is a three-candle imbalance where candles 1 and 3 wicks do not overlap"
- Claim B (Source 2, Mechanistic): "Fair Value Gaps are price inefficiencies created when price moves without trading in a zone"
- Claim C (Source 3, Predictive): "FVGs form when price gaps over support/resistance levels without filling the gap"

Claim Landscape Assessment:
- Claim A: Structural definition (Definitional)
- Claim B: Mechanism description (Mechanistic)
- Claim C: Outcome/behavior description (Predictive)
- All three are compatible; they describe different aspects of the same phenomenon

Interpretation Construction:
- Foundation: All three claims inform the core understanding
- Synthesis: "A Fair Value Gap is defined structurally as a three-candle imbalance where candles 1 and 3 wicks do not overlap (Definitional, Claim A). This structure represents a price inefficiency mechanism where price moves without trading in a zone (Mechanistic, Claim B), and typically results in gap-fill behavior (Predictive, Claim C). The interpretation integrates these three complementary aspects while preserving their distinct semantic roles."
- Provisional status: Not provisional; consensus across sources
- Framework handling: ✓ Synthesizes multiple compatible claims into unified interpretation while preserving A3 semantic roles

---

**Test 2 — Multiple compatible variations**

Concept: Order Block
Source Claims:
- Claim A (Source 1): "Order Blocks form from bearish consolidation before bullish impulses"
- Claim B (Source 2): "Order Blocks form from the last consolidation before any impulse, regardless of direction"
- Claim C (Source 3): "Order Blocks are consolidations that precede significant price movements; the specific impulse direction varies"

Claim Landscape Assessment:
- Claim A: Specific (bearish consolidation → bullish impulse)
- Claim B: General (consolidation → any impulse)
- Claim C: General with variations
- Tension: Claim A is more specific; Claims B and C are broader. Not contradictory, but varying scope

Interpretation Construction:
- Foundation: All three claims; Claim A is more specific, Claims B and C capture breadth
- Synthesis: "An Order Block is a consolidation that precedes an impulse. While impulses can be directional (Claim A's bearish-before-bullish pattern) or bidirectional (Claims B, C), the core definition is the consolidation-impulse relationship. Sources suggest the bearish-to-bullish pattern is common but not universal."
- Provisional status: Not provisional; the variation is resolvable as scope differences
- Framework handling: ✓ Interpretation encompasses variations by distinguishing core definition from scope variations

---

**Test 3 — Unresolved contradiction**

Concept: Optimal Trade Entry
Source Claims:
- Claim A (Source 1): "Optimal Trade Entry occurs at the 61.8% retracement of the impulse"
- Claim B (Source 2): "Optimal Trade Entry occurs at the 50% retracement"
- Claim C (Source 3): "Optimal Trade Entry varies based on impulse strength; typically 50–70% retracement"

Claim Landscape Assessment:
- Claim A and B: Conflicting specific numbers (61.8% vs. 50%)
- Claim C: Range acknowledgment
- Tension: Multiple sources assert different specific entry levels; Claim C acknowledges variation but does not fully resolve the conflict

Contradiction Analysis (A5): Is this a genuine contradiction?
- Claim A asserts 61.8% specifically
- Claim B asserts 50% specifically
- If applied to the same impulse under the same conditions, both cannot be optimal
- Genuine contradiction (unresolved per A5)

Interpretation Construction:
- Foundation: All three claims; acknowledge that sources conflict on the specific level
- Interpretation (provisional): "Optimal Trade Entry is provisionally interpreted as occurring in the 50–70% retracement range (Claim C), which encompasses but does not resolve the contradiction between 50% (Claim B) and 61.8% (Claim A). The interpretation adopts the broader range while explicitly preserving the unresolved contradiction about the specific optimal level. Further empirical testing or source clarification is required to resolve which specific level, if either, is optimal."
- Provisional status: Yes — explicitly acknowledges the unresolved contradiction
- Framework handling: ✓ Interpretation acknowledges unresolved contradiction without forcing false reconciliation; remains authoritative despite provisionality

---

**Test 4 — Genuine unresolved contradiction**

Concept: Market Maker Intent
Source Claims:
- Claim A (Source 1): "Market Makers actively hunt stop-losses to accumulate liquidity for their trades"
- Claim B (Source 2): "Market Makers' stop-loss hunting is a side effect of their natural order flow, not intentional"

Claim Landscape Assessment:
- Direct logical incompatibility: intentional vs. unintentional behavior
- Applied to the same market conditions and same Market Makers
- No context resolves this contradiction (A5 Step 2)
- Genuine unresolved contradiction per A5

Interpretation Construction:
- Foundation: Both claims present; researcher acknowledges the contradiction
- Interpretation (bracketed contradiction): "The interpretation of Market Maker Intent is that Market Makers' behavior creates stop-loss hunting effects (both sources agree on the behavior). However, sources disagree on whether this is intentional (Claim A) or a side effect of natural order flow (Claim B). The interpretation provisionally treats it as intentional behavior while explicitly preserving the contradiction about intent."
- Provisional status: Yes — explicitly brackets and preserves the unresolved contradiction
- Researcher choice: The researcher has selected intentionality as the provisional foundation, but justified the choice by acknowledging the competing claim
- Framework handling: ✓ Interpretation does not force reconciliation; does not silently dismiss Claim B; makes an authoritative choice while preserving the contradiction

---

**Test 5 — Researcher selecting one interpretation over competing claims**

Concept: Liquidity Void
Source Claims:
- Claim A (Source 1, highly reputable): "Liquidity Voids are zones without market participants; price will eventually fill them"
- Claim B (Source 2, lower provenance confidence): "Liquidity Voids are temporary phenomena that often close without price filling the zone"

Claim Landscape Assessment:
- Competing claims about the same phenomenon
- A5 status: Assume A5 classified this as Tension rather than genuine contradiction (timeframe dependency may explain the difference: "eventually" vs. "often" suggests probabilistic or temporal variation)
- Tension: Source A asserts voids will fill; Source B asserts they often close without filling
- Source A has higher authority/reputation

Interpretation Construction (NOT RECOMMENDED):
- If reasoning is: "Source A is more reputable, therefore adopt Claim A as interpretation"
- Framework handling: ✗ Violates Rule 10 (source authority does not override reasoning)

Interpretation Construction (CORRECT):
- Foundation: Acknowledge both claims; reason from available landscape
- Interpretation: "Liquidity Voids are zones without market participants. The interpretation acknowledges tension in source material about their resolution: Source A asserts they eventually fill; Source B asserts they often close without filling. The distinction may depend on timeframe or market conditions not yet fully specified in source material. The interpretation provisionally treats voids as structures that will eventually fill (adopting Claim A's position) while acknowledging that on shorter timeframes they may appear to close without filling (Claim B's observation). This provisional synthesis awaits empirical clarification of timeframe-dependent behavior."
- Provisional status: Yes — acknowledges tension and dependence on unspecified conditions
- Framework handling: ✓ Researcher makes a choice but justifies it by reasoning about conditions, not by source authority alone; preserves competing claim; makes reasoned assertion

---

**Test 6 — Researcher constructing interpretation beyond what any single source states**

Concept: Institutional Trading Pattern Recognition
Source Claims:
- Claim A (Source 1): "Institutions create Order Blocks through consolidation"
- Claim B (Source 2): "Institutions create Fair Value Gaps through rapid price movement"
- Claim C (Source 3): "Institutions use both consolidation and rapid movement to manage liquidity"

Claim Landscape Assessment:
- All three claims are compatible; each describes part of institutional behavior
- No single source states a complete integrated framework
- Researcher synthesis is possible and appropriate

Interpretation Construction:
- Foundation: All three claims; researcher integrates them
- Synthesis (researcher-derived): "Institutional Trading Pattern Recognition in this framework encompasses two primary mechanisms: Order Blocks (consolidation-based liquidity management, Claim A) and Fair Value Gaps (movement-based liquidity management, Claim B). These represent complementary tactics within an integrated liquidity-management strategy (Claim C). The interpretation synthesizes these into a two-mechanism framework."
- Provisional status: Not provisional; synthesis is well-supported by the available claim landscape
- Researcher contribution: Integration of three distinct claims into a unified framework
- Framework handling: ✓ Researcher goes beyond single-source assertion but remains grounded in available claim landscape; reasoning is traceable and authoritative

---

**Test 7 — New relevant source material appearing after interpretation**

Researcher has established interpretation (from Test 6):
"Institutional Trading Pattern Recognition encompasses Order Blocks and Fair Value Gaps as complementary liquidity-management mechanisms."

New Source Material:
- Claim D (Source 4, discovered after interpretation): "Institutions also use psychological price levels (round numbers) as a third liquidity-management mechanism, distinct from Order Blocks and FVGs"

Interpretation Evolution Trigger:
- New material is materially relevant (addresses institutional patterns)
- New material is domain-relevant per A2-C1
- New material cannot be ignored without violating Rule 2 (account for available claim landscape)
- This situation triggers a need for interpretation evolution

Framework handling: ✓ A6 acknowledges that new materially relevant source material creates a condition where interpretation evolution becomes appropriate. The procedure and triggers for such evolution are A7 territory.

---

**Test 8 — Researcher changing interpretation without new source material**

Researcher's Prior Interpretation:
"Order Blocks require bearish consolidation before bullish impulses"

Researcher's Revised Interpretation (same sources):
"Order Blocks form from any consolidation before any impulse direction; the bearish-before-bullish pattern is frequent but not universal"

Change Basis:
- Not new source material
- Re-examination of existing available claim landscape
- Researcher has shifted synthesis of existing claims (Test 2 situation)
- No new evidence, no new sources

Interpretation Evolution Trigger:
- This is a researcher judgment shift, not forced by new material
- Permitted per D1 Rule 3 (interpretation may evolve)
- The conditions and procedure for such evolution are A7 territory

Framework handling: ✓ A6 acknowledges that researcher re-examination of the available claim landscape may lead to judgment evolution. The triggers and procedure for formal revision are A7 territory.

---

- **B2 (testability criteria), M2 (hypothesis formation), and downstream research work** can now reference the researcher's authoritative reference interpretation of each concept as a foundation.

SEMANTIC CONSISTENCY AUDIT — DECISION A6
Upstream Authority:

PROJECT MINORE — FOUNDING DEFINITION v0.1 (RATIFIED)
DECISION D1 — CONCEPT UNDERSTANDING: RESEARCHER INTERPRETATION LIFECYCLE (RATIFIED)
DECISION A1 — RESEARCH INPUT ADMISSIBILITY AND PROVENANCE (RATIFIED)
DECISION A2 — CLAIM EXTRACTION PROCEDURE (RATIFIED)
A2-C1 and A2-C2 (BINDING)
DECISION A3 — CLAIM CLASSIFICATION (RATIFIED)
A3-C1 (BINDING)
DECISION A4 — CONCEPT ASSOCIATION (RATIFIED)
DECISION A5 — CONTRADICTION HANDLING PROCEDURE (RATIFIED)
DECISION A6 — RESEARCHER INTERPRETATION CONSTRUCTION (PROPOSED, under audit)
Point 1 Finding — "Complete Claim Landscape"
Exact finding:

A6 Step 1 requires: "Identify all extracted source claims associated with the concept per A4."

A6 Rule 2 requires: "The researcher must consider all materially relevant source claims."

The word "all" appears to create an absolute completeness standard.

Upstream authority test:

No ratified decision establishes that the researcher must have perfect knowledge of all possible claims that could exist anywhere. A1 admits research inputs as they are discovered; A2 extracts claims from admitted inputs; A4 associates them with concepts.

The claim landscape is necessarily bounded by what has been admitted, extracted, and associated at the time of interpretation construction.

Necessity test:

Does A6 require absolute omniscience, or only accountability for the currently available processed claims?

The protective requirement is: the researcher must not silently dismiss available material evidence. The requirement is not that undiscovered material must be discovered before interpretation is permitted.

Whether a real inconsistency exists:

Yes. "Complete claim landscape" and "all materially relevant claims" create an impossible epistemic standard if interpreted as requiring knowledge of undiscovered sources.

Minimum correction required:

Replace "complete claim landscape" with "available claim landscape" throughout.

Clarify Step 1: "Identify all extracted source claims currently associated with the concept per A4."

Clarify Rule 2: "The researcher must consider all materially relevant source claims in the available claim landscape. Silent dismissal of available claims is prohibited."

Classification: CORRECTION REQUIRED

Point 2 Finding — Reliability Weighting in Step 3
Exact finding:

A6 Step 3 permits foundation-selection exclusion based on: "Explicit judgment that a claim is less reliable" and states: "the researcher may weigh evidence of reliability."

Upstream authority test:

A1 establishes provenance uncertainty but does not disqualify claims (Rule 3)
A5 establishes that authority does not resolve contradiction (Rule 8)
No ratified decision establishes a reliability-weighting framework
Semantic distinction test:

What does "reliability" mean in A6's usage?

Provenance confidence (A1 territory — already handled)
Source authority (A5 explicitly rejects this as a resolution mechanism)
Truth of the claim (empirical validation — not yet reached)
Interpretive judgment about which claims are more foundational (legitimate researcher authority)
The term "reliability" conflates these dimensions without definition.

Necessity test:

Does A6 need reliability weighting, or only explicit reasoning for interpretive choices?

The protective requirement is: the researcher must explicitly reason about why certain claims inform the interpretation and others do not. The requirement is not to introduce a reliability metric.

A6 already permits exclusion based on: "relevance to core concept vs. peripheral variations" and "scope or applicability conditions." These are sufficient without introducing "reliability."

Whether a real inconsistency exists:

Yes. "Reliability" introduces an undefined weighting dimension that may silently import source authority, truth claims, or provenance confidence without proper semantic grounding.

Minimum correction required:

Remove "explicit judgment that a claim is less reliable" from the permitted exclusion list.

Remove "the researcher may weigh evidence of reliability."

Retain only: relevance to core concept, scope/applicability conditions, and competing claims that appear more foundational (with explicit reasoning required for all exclusions).

Classification: CORRECTION REQUIRED

Point 3 Finding — "Binding on Downstream Research Work"
Exact finding:

A6 defines authoritative interpretation as: "binding on downstream research decisions until the researcher explicitly revises it" (Step 6) and "binding on downstream research work" (Rule 6).

Upstream authority test:

D1 establishes that the researcher holds a single authoritative interpretation (Rule 2) but does not state that this interpretation is binding on downstream research in the sense of preventing questioning or empirical testing.

Semantic distinction test:

What does "binding" legitimately mean?

The current reference point for downstream work (valid — downstream research must know what the current interpretation is)
Mandatory constraint preventing downstream research from questioning or testing the interpretation (invalid — would prevent B1 from identifying research questions about the interpretation; would prevent empirical falsification)
Necessity test:

Does "binding" serve A6's purpose, or does it overreach into constraining future research?

The protective requirement is: downstream research must reference the current interpretation and make explicit when departing from it. The requirement is not that the interpretation is unquestionable.

Whether a real inconsistency exists:

Yes. "Binding" is semantically ambiguous and may be read as preventing downstream research from questioning the interpretation, which would contradict the research process itself (empirical testing may contradict interpretations).

Minimum correction required:

Replace "binding on downstream research work" with: "the authoritative reference interpretation for downstream research work. Downstream research references this interpretation unless explicitly departing with documented reasoning."

Remove the word "binding" from Step 6 and Rule 6.

Classification: CORRECTION REQUIRED

Point 4 Finding — Test 1 and Semantic Laundering
Exact finding:

Test 1 combines:

Claim A: Definitional (structural definition)
Claim B: Mechanistic (mechanism description)
Claim C: Predictive (outcome/behavior)
into a unified interpretation that synthesizes all three into what a Fair Value Gap is.

Upstream authority test:

A3 establishes that classification determines what kind of assertion the source made. Classification is preserved and must not be altered (A3 Rule 2).

A6 Rule 7 states: "Interpretation does not alter source claims."

Semantic distinction test:

Does combining a Mechanistic claim and a Predictive claim into a Definitional interpretation alter the semantic role of those claims?

Claim B states how FVGs work (mechanism). Claim C states what happens with FVGs (prediction). Test 1's interpretation treats both as part of what FVGs are (definition).

This is semantic role transformation: a mechanistic explanation is being incorporated into a definitional understanding. The claim itself is not altered, but its semantic role in the interpretation is shifted.

Necessity test:

May a researcher's interpretation synthesize claims with different A3 classifications into a unified understanding?

Yes — this is legitimate researcher synthesis. The error is not the synthesis itself but the failure to preserve the semantic distinction between:

What FVGs structurally are (Definitional claims)
How they work (Mechanistic claims)
What they predict (Predictive claims)
The interpretation should make clear which aspects are definitional, which are mechanistic, and which are predictive, even when synthesizing them.

Whether a real inconsistency exists:

Yes. Test 1 silently launders Mechanistic and Predictive claims into a Definitional synthesis without preserving their distinct semantic roles.

Minimum correction required:

Revise Test 1 interpretation to:

"A Fair Value Gap is defined structurally as a three-candle imbalance where candles 1 and 3 wicks do not overlap (Definitional, Claim A). This structure represents a price inefficiency mechanism (Mechanistic, Claim B) that typically results in gap-fill behavior (Predictive, Claim C). The interpretation integrates these three complementary aspects while preserving their distinct semantic roles."

Add a binding rule: "Interpretation synthesis must preserve the semantic roles of claims as classified per A3. Mechanistic and Predictive claims may inform concept understanding without being transformed into Definitional claims."

Classification: CORRECTION REQUIRED

Point 5 Finding — Test 3 and Hidden Reconciliation
Exact finding:

Test 3 identifies:

Claim A: 61.8% (specific)
Claim B: 50% (specific)
Claim C: 50–70% range
Test 3 concludes: "Genuine contradiction (unresolved per A5)" between A and B.

Then constructs interpretation: "provisionally interpreted as occurring in the 50–70% retracement range."

Upstream authority test:

A5 Rule 5: "Contradictions are preserved as unresolved until the researcher explicitly reconciles them."

A6 Rule 5: "If A5 identifies a genuine unresolved contradiction, the interpretation may provisionally adopt a position, but the contradiction must be explicitly preserved in the interpretation reasoning chain."

Logical analysis:

Adopting the 50–70% range appears to reconcile the contradiction by absorbing both specific claims into a broader assertion. This is reconciliation by generalization.

However, A6 Rule 5 permits provisional positioning. The question is whether the 50–70% range is:

A provisional position that preserves the contradiction (valid)
A synthesis that makes the contradiction appear resolved (invalid)
Test 3's wording states: "The tension between these specific levels remains unresolved." This preserves the contradiction.

Whether a real inconsistency exists:

No, but clarity is inadequate. Test 3 does preserve the unresolved contradiction, but the interpretation's adoption of the range could be misread as resolving it.

Minimum correction required:

Strengthen Test 3's interpretation wording to make preservation more explicit:

"Optimal Trade Entry is provisionally interpreted as occurring in the 50–70% retracement range (Claim C), which encompasses but does not resolve the contradiction between 50% (Claim B) and 61.8% (Claim A). The interpretation adopts the broader range while explicitly preserving the unresolved contradiction about the specific optimal level."

Classification: NARROW CLARIFICATION

Point 6 Finding — Test 5 and A5 Authority
Exact finding:

Test 5 states: "No unresolved contradiction per A5 (both could be true under different conditions if qualified)."

The claims are:

Claim A: "Liquidity Voids... will eventually fill"
Claim B: "Liquidity Voids... often close without price filling the zone"
Upstream authority test:

A5 determines contradiction status via a four-step procedure ending in Step 4 (declare genuine contradiction) or not.

A6 does not have authority to re-perform or override A5's contradiction determination.

Logical analysis:

"Will eventually fill" vs. "often close without filling" — are these contradictory?

Without context:

"Will eventually fill" (universal, temporal)
"Often close without filling" (probabilistic exception)
Under A5 Step 2, context must be applied. If Claim B's "often close" means they permanently close without filling (not merely delayed filling), then there is a contradiction.

If Claim B means they appear to close but eventually fill anyway, there is no contradiction.

Necessity test:

Does A6 need to independently determine contradiction status, or must it inherit status from A5?

A6 must inherit. Contradiction determination is A5 territory. A6 operates on the claim landscape as delivered by A5.

Whether a real inconsistency exists:

Yes. A6 is performing contradiction determination (an A5 function) within an interpretation test. Test 5 should state: "Assuming A5 determined this is not a genuine contradiction..." or should acknowledge that A5's contradiction determination is a prerequisite input.

Additionally, Test 5's parenthetical reasoning ("both could be true under different conditions if qualified") is performing A5 Step 2 (context application) — which is not A6's role.

Minimum correction required:

Revise Test 5:

Remove: "No unresolved contradiction per A5 (both could be true under different conditions if qualified)."

Replace with: "A5 contradiction determination status: [If A5 determined contradiction exists, interpret accordingly; if not, proceed as Tension/Variation]. For the purpose of this test, assume A5 classified this as Tension rather than genuine contradiction."

Alternatively, replace Test 5 entirely with a clearer example where A5 status is unambiguous.

Classification: CORRECTION REQUIRED

Point 7 Finding — A6 vs. A7 Scope Boundary
Exact finding:

A6 includes:

Rule 11: "Interpretation evolution is preserved. When interpretation changes, the prior version is preserved... The reasoning for the change is documented."
Test 7: Describes new source material triggering revision, documents prior version preservation, states reasoning for revision
Test 8: Describes researcher judgment shift without new material, documents reasoning for change, preserves prior version
Upstream authority test:

D1 already establishes:

Rule 3: "The researcher's interpretation may evolve over time."
Rule 4: "Prior interpretations are preserved as historical provenance, not overwritten."
A7 is separately designated for Interpretation Evolution Triggers.

Semantic distinction test:

What is A6's legitimate scope vs. A7's scope?

A6 must establish: how to construct an interpretation initially.

A7 should establish: under what conditions an existing interpretation is revised, and what procedure governs that revision.

Rule 11, Test 7, and Test 8 are defining:

Evolution triggers (new material, researcher judgment shift)
Revision procedure (preserve prior, document reasoning)
Version transition mechanics
These are A7 content, not A6 construction content.

Necessity test:

Does A6 need any of this content to remain coherent?

A6 only needs to acknowledge that interpretations may later evolve (already stated in D1). It does not need to define triggers, procedures, or version mechanics.

Tests 7 and 8 are legitimate adversarial tests of A6's interpretation construction procedure under edge conditions (new material appears; researcher changes mind). However, they venture into A7 territory by defining what happens during evolution.

Whether a real inconsistency exists:

Yes. A6 has crossed into A7 scope by defining evolution triggers, preservation procedures, and reasoning requirements for change.

Minimum correction required:

Remove Rule 11 entirely (defer to A7).

Revise Test 7 to stop at: "New material is materially relevant and cannot be ignored without violating Rule 2. This situation would trigger interpretation evolution (A7 territory)." Do not define the revision procedure.

Revise Test 8 to stop at: "Researcher judgment shift is permitted per D1 Rule 3. The conditions and procedure for such evolution are A7 territory." Do not define the documentation requirements.

Retain only the minimal acknowledgment in A6 that interpretations constructed per A6 may later be subject to evolution per A7.

Classification: REMOVE / DEFER TO A7

Upstream authority test:

D1 Rule 2: "At any given time, the researcher holds a single authoritative interpretation of a concept, which constitutes the current understanding for that concept."

This directly answers the question: no, multiple competing researcher interpretations do not coexist as current authoritative interpretations. There is one current interpretation per concept.

Semantic distinction test:

Multiple historical prior interpretations (exist as versioned provenance)
Multiple current authoritative interpretations (D1 prohibits this)
Necessity test:

Only if "multiple researchers" (Project Minore Foundation defines the primary user as one researcher). Even if the system later supports multiple researchers, each researcher would still hold one current interpretation per D1.

Whether a real inconsistency exists:

Yes. The question is already resolved by D1. Listing it as unresolved contradicts ratified upstream authority.
