PROJECT MINORE — FOUNDING DEFINITION v0.1
STATUS
Ratified

PURPOSE

Project Minore is a personal research system for transforming trading knowledge into structured, testable, evidence-based trading knowledge.

The system exists because trading education often mixes:

source claims
definitions
interpretations
mechanisms
predictions
personal beliefs
trading rules
empirical results
without clearly separating them.

Project Minore must preserve these distinctions.

CORE PROBLEM

The user studies complex trading frameworks, especially ICT-related material, but the knowledge is distributed across sources and often presented without a reliable dependency structure.

Learning more material does not automatically produce a better trading model.

The actual problem is the absence of a controlled research process that can answer:

What exactly did the source claim?
What concept is the claim about?
What does the concept mean across all relevant source material?
Which parts are definitions, mechanisms, predictions, or heuristics?
Which claims can be tested?
What does market data actually support or contradict?
Which findings are reliable enough to influence the user's trading model?
How does the model evolve when new evidence appears?
MISSION

Project Minore must create a traceable path from:

SOURCE MATERIAL
→ EXTRACTED KNOWLEDGE
→ STRUCTURED UNDERSTANDING
→ RESEARCH QUESTIONS
→ TESTABLE HYPOTHESES
→ EMPIRICAL TESTS
→ RESULTS
→ EVIDENCE
→ TRADING MODEL DEVELOPMENT

The exact architecture of this path is NOT decided by this document.

CORE PRINCIPLE

Project Minore must never confuse:

what a source said
what the source may have meant
what the researcher currently believes
what can be tested
what has actually been tested
what empirical evidence supports
what the user chooses to use in the trading model
These are different epistemic states and must remain distinguishable throughout the system.

PRIMARY USER

The initial primary user is one independent trader-researcher.

Project Minore is not initially designed as:

a public trading platform
a signal service
a broker
a social network
a course platform
an automated money-management system
Its initial purpose is personal research and trading-model development.

PRIMARY RESEARCH OBJECT

The initial research domain is ICT-related trading knowledge.

However, Project Minore must not assume that ICT claims are true.

ICT material is research input.

Market evidence determines empirical support.

The system may later incorporate:

price and market data
macroeconomic data
DXY and intermarket relationships
yields and interest rates
seasonal data
COT data
external research
These are future research inputs, not initial architecture requirements.

CORE CAPABILITIES

At minimum, the completed system should eventually support:

Registering and organizing research sources
Preserving provenance of extracted source material
Structuring trading concepts and their relationships
Separating source claims from researcher interpretation
Building an evolving understanding of concepts
Identifying unanswered research questions
Formulating falsifiable hypotheses
Defining reproducible tests
Running or recording experiments and backtests
Preserving quantitative results
Evaluating evidence without confusing source authority with empirical validation
Evolving the user's trading model from accumulated evidence
Preserving the full reasoning and provenance chain behind important conclusions

system architecture

entity names or cardinalities
These decisions must be derived later from validated requirements.

AI may eventually assist with:

source analysis
extraction

research synthesis

claude-sonnet-5-high
This is a clear, disciplined document. Before treating it as ratified, here's a structured review — strengths, gaps, and open questions worth resolving at the foundation stage (since ambiguities here will propagate into every later architectural decision).

What's Working Well
Epistemic separation is the real spine of this document, not the pipeline diagram. The pipeline (source → extraction → ... → model) is just the visible consequence of maintaining that separation. That's the right ordering of priorities.
Non-goals section is doing real work — explicitly deferring schema/architecture prevents premature structural commitments that would be hard to unwind later.
Success condition is falsifiable in spirit: "a rule with no traceable lineage is not a fully researched rule" gives you an actual test you can apply to your own future work, not just an aspiration.
AI role constraint is correctly conservative — AI output defaulting to non-canonical until researcher acceptance avoids the most common failure mode of AI-assisted research systems (silent authority laundering).
Gaps Worth Addressing Before Ratification
1. Contradiction handling has no home.
The document mentions "contradiction detection" as an AI capability, but contradiction itself isn't named as a first-class epistemic state. ICT sources routinely conflict (different mentors define "order block" differently, or a source contradicts itself over time). Does Minore:

treat each source's claim as scoped to that source only (no forced reconciliation), or
require an explicit reconciliation step before a concept has "structured understanding"?
This affects whether "structured understanding of a concept" is singular or must support multiple competing versions simultaneously.

2. No principle for evidence strength/grading.
The success condition says "how strong that evidence is" — but nothing in the core principles establishes what makes evidence strong (sample size, out-of-sample validation, robustness across regimes, overfitting risk). Without at least a stated intention here, "strength" risks becoming subjective at the exact point where the system is supposed to prevent subjectivity from masquerading as rigor.

3. Failed hypotheses / negative results have no explicit status.
The pipeline implies hypotheses get tested and produce evidence, but doesn't say whether a failed test is preserved as first-class negative evidence (which future you should not silently re-test) or just... disappears. Given the mission's evolution clause ("how does the model evolve when new evidence appears"), negative evidence seems just as important as positive.

4. Interpretation drift over time isn't addressed.
The researcher's understanding of a source will change as more sources are read. Does an old "researcher interpretation" get versioned/superseded, or overwritten? This matters because the system promises full reasoning/provenance preservation — silently overwriting a past interpretation would violate that even if unintentional.

5. "Concept" is load-bearing but undefined.
Not asking for a schema (respecting the non-goals), but worth flagging: concepts are the thing claims attach to, hypotheses are about, and the model is built from. At some point before architecture work starts, you'll need at least an informal working definition of what counts as a "concept" vs. a "claim about a concept" vs. a "relationship between concepts."

Suggested Next Step
Rather than folding fixes into v0.1 directly, I'd recommend either:

(a) a short "Open Questions" appendix to this document capturing the five points above as acknowledged unresolved issues (keeps v0.1 honest about its own incompleteness without scope creep), or
(b) moving straight to a v0.2 that resolves just #1 and #2 (contradiction handling and evidence strength), since those two most directly affect whether "empirical support" as a concept even means anything yet.
Which direction do you want to take — patch this document, or treat it as ratified as-is and move to defining requirements for the next layer?
